Study B-13.098
B-13.098 studied the inhibition potential of aprocitentan on P-gp activity in Caco-2 cells using
digoxin as a prototypical substrate. Concentrations of aprocitentan ranged from 3 to 70μΜ.
Higher concentrations could not be studied due to limited solubility in the transport buffer. The
absorptive permeability of digoxin increased in a concentration-dependent manner, as in the
presence of 3μΜ and 70μΜ of aprocitentan, digoxin permeability increased by 1% and 35%,
respectively.
Study B-20.030
B-20.030 assessed the inhibition potential of aprocitentan on the activity of OATP1B1 and
OATP1B3 transporters in an assay with a pre-incubation period in CHO cells expressing each
transporter. Aprocitentan inhibited OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 mediated uptake of atorvastatin
acid with IC50 values of 19 and 27µM, respectively. Cyclosporin A was used as a control
inhibitor, and inhibited uptake of atorvastatin acid with IC50 values of 0.1 and 0.3µM for
OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, respectively. Nonspecific binding was assessed in CHO wild-type
cells at concentrations of 19 and 27µM, and the fraction recovered from incubation solutions was
45% and 83%.
Study B-17.069
B-17.069 assessed the potential of aprocitentan to alter the enzymatic activity of CYP2C8 and
CYP2C19 due to time-dependent inhibition, as well as the inhibition potential of aprocitentan on
the activity of UGT1A1 and UGT2B7. Experiments used human liver microsomes for CYP2C8,
recombinant CYP2C19, and recombinant UGT enzymes for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7. The marker
reactions for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 were estradiol 3-β-glucuronidation and 3’-azido-3’145
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
deoxythymidine 5-β-glucuronidation, respectively. Paclitaxel 6α-hydroxylation was used as a
marker reaction for CYP2C8, and formation of 4-hydroxymephenytoin was monitored as a
marker for CYP2C19 activity. Gemfibrozil 1-O-β glucuronide and ticlopidine were reference
inhibitors of CYP2C8 and CYP2C19, respectively. IC50 shift was determined as the ratio of the
IC50 values without and with NADPH and indicates the change in enzyme activity during the
pre-incubation period. The IC50 shift for aprocitentan in CYP2C8 was 1.0 and was not
determined in CYP2C19 because the IC50 with pre-incubation with and without NADPH were
both >100µM, whereas the IC50 shift for gemfibrozil 1-O-β -glucuronide and ticlopidine for
CYP2C8 and CYP2C19 was 24 and 7.8, respectively. This indicates that aprocitentan is not a
time-dependent inhibitor of CYP2C8 and CYP2C19. Aprocitentan was a weak inhibitor of
UGT1A1 and UGT2B7, with IC50 values of 23 and 43μΜ, respectively. Nonspecific binding of
aprocitentan in the UGT inhibition assays was 3.9% and 3.8% for UGT1A1 and UGT2B7,
respectively.
Study B-21.017
B-21.017 assessed the time-dependent inhibition of CYP1A1 and CYP2B6 by aprocitentan in
human liver microsomes. Marker reactions for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 were phenacetin-Odeethylation and bupropion hydroxylation, respectively. Furafylline and ticlopidine were run in
parallel as positive controls for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6, respectively. IC50 shift was determined
as the ratio of the IC50 values without and with NADHP and indicate the change in enzyme
activity during the pre-incubation period. The IC50 shift for aprocitentan in CYP1A2 and
CYP2B6 was not determined, as values with and without NADPH were >100µM, whereas the
IC50 shift for furafylline and ticlopidine for CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 was 94 and 131, respectively.
This indicates that aprocitentan is not a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP1A2 and CYP2B6.
Study B-05.107
B-05.107 assessed the potential of aprocitentan to induce human CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and
CYP1A2. Activation of the human pregnane X receptor (PXR) by aprocitentan was tested in a
reporter gene assay, and induction of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2 was tested in three
batches of primary human hepatocytes on an mRNA and/or enzyme activity level. Nifedipine,
diclofenac, and phenacetin were used as marker substrates for CYP3A4, CYP2C9, and CYP1A2
activity, respectively. Rifampicin was a positive control in the PXR activation assay.
Aprocitentan was tested for induction at concentration up to 10µM. The positive control for the
CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 regulation assays was rifampicin, and the positive control for the
CYP1A2 regulation assay was omeprazole.
Aprocitentan activated PXR with a half maximal effective concentration (EC50) of 7.2 to 8.7µM,
which is less potent than the positive control rifampicin (EC50 0.5 to 0.6µM). Aprocitentan
demonstrated generally concentration-dependent increases in CYP3A4 mRNA and enzyme
activity. In the first batch of hepatocytes, the fold activity was 1.36, 1.57, 2.90, and 3.87 at 0.1, 1,
3, and 10µM, respectively. In the second batch of hepatocytes, the fold activity was 0.99, 1.13,
1.42, and 1.36 at 0.1, 1, 3, and 10µM, respectively. In the third batch of hepatocytes, the fold
activity was 1.35, 1.47, 1.51, and 1.28 at 0.1, 1, 3, and 10µM, respectively. CYP3A4 mRNA
levels also increased dose dependently, with 10µM producing a 6.72-, 2.77-, and 2.84-fold
induction in each hepatocyte batch compared to a 9.59-, 5.30-, and 4.89-fold induction in each
146
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
hepatocyte batch for 10µM rifampicin. There were no dose-dependent increases in CYP2C9
activity; however, it does appear that there was a small dose-dependent increase in CYP2C9
mRNA levels. The 10µM dose produced a 1.59-, 1.25-, and 1.29-fold induction in each
hepatocyte batch compared to a 2.42-, 2.04-, and 2.90-fold induction at 10µM rifampicin. There
were dose-dependent increases in CYP1A2 activity with increasing aprocitentan dose. The
10µM dose produced a 3.05-, 2.28- and 2.71-fold increase in activity, compared to a 19.73-,
14.23-, and 10.83-fold increase in activity with omeprazole.
Study B-12.103
B-12.103 assessed the potential for aprocitentan to competitively inhibit CYP2C8 and timedependent inhibition of CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 in vitro using human liver microsomes and
cytochrome P450 isoform-specific marker transformations. The reactions used paclitaxel 6αhydroxylation, diclofenac 4’-hydroxylation, dextromethorphan-O-demethylation, and
testosterone 6β-hydroxylation as marker reactions for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2D6, and
CYP3A4, respectively. Reference inhibitors included quercetin, tienilic acid, paroxetine, and
mibefradil for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 respectively.
Aprocitentan was an inhibitor of CYP2C8 activity (IC50=23µM). Aprocitentan had no timedependent inhibition of CYP2C9, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4 activities, as the shift values were 1.2,
0.9, and not calculable due to absence of inhibition, respectively, compared to reference shift
values of 190, 64, and 29, respectively.
Study B-13.097
Study B-13.097 assessed the inhibition potential of aprocitentan on the activity of efflux and
uptake organic anion transporters (OATs) OAT1 and OAT3, organic cation transporters (OCTs)
OCT1 and OCT2, multidrug and toxin extrusion transporters (MATE)-1 and MATE2K, and
BCRP. Probe substrates (µM) were MPP+ (5), pulmonary arterial hypertension (10), furosemide
(5), MPP+ (5), metformin (50), and 4-(4-dimethylaminostyryl)-N-methylpyridinium (1), and
positive control inhibitors (µM) were repaglinide (600), probenecid (100), probenecid (100),
imipramine (300), cimetidine (100), and ondansetron (100) for OCT-1, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2,
MATE1, and MATE2K, respectively. For BCRP assessment in BCRP-MDCK cells, cladribine
was used as a probe substrate, and Ko143 was used as a positive control inhibitor.
Aprocitentan showed inhibitory potential for OAT1 (IC50=4.7µM). Aprocitentan also showed
inhibitory potential of BCRP (IC50=5.7µM). Aprocitentan had some inhibition in a
concentration-dependent manner for OAT3 (IC50=1.5µM). There was no effect observed on
OCT1, OCT2, or MATE2K or MATE1 at the concentrations tested (up to 100µM).

14.1.5. In Vitro Summary
Plasma protein binding of aprocitentan in humans was 99.5%, and the mean blood/plasma ratio
was 0.54, indicating limited binding to red blood cells. Aprocitentan was highly permeable
across cell membranes. When studies were conducted to determine if aprocitentan is dependent
on hepatic uptake transporters OATP1B1 and OATP1B3, the uptake of aprocitentan in wild-type
and OATP-expressing cells was high and rapid without much difference between the two cell
147
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
line types, indicating that hepatic uptake of aprocitentan is mostly due to passive diffusion.
Aprocitentan is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP; however, aprocitentan has high permeability
across cell membranes, and PK was dose-proportional up to 100 mg, indicating a limited role for
efflux transporters in oral absorption. Further, aprocitentan was mostly metabolized prior to
excretion. Therefore, impact on aprocitentan exposure due to P-gp or BCRP inhibitors affecting
absorption or excretion is not expected. Only 0.2% of the administered dose was excreted
unchanged in the urine, so change in uptake and efflux by renal transporter inhibitors or inducers
would not likely have clinical relevance.
Aprocitentan inhibited uptake of several model substrates for several drug transport proteins,
with the strongest inhibition seen on OAT1/3 and BCRP (IC50 values ranging from 1.5 to
5.7µM). Inhibition of OATP, NTCP, and bile salt export pump was also observed, but to a lesser
extent (IC50 values ranging from 14 to 50µM). IC50 values for P-gp, OCT1/2, or MATE1/2K were
around or above the highest tested concentration. It was determined in vitro that aprocitentan
does not inhibit P-gp because the intestinal luminal concentration of the interacting drug/IC50 is
1.83 for a 25 mg dose of aprocitentan, which is less than the threshold of 10. BCRP showed
potential to inhibit BCRP in vitro because the intestinal luminal concentration of the interacting
drug/IC50 value was 32 for a 25 mg dose of aprocitentan, which is greater than the threshold of
10. A clinical drug-drug interaction study for BCRP was conducted (see Section 14.2). R values
for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 were 1.007 and 1.003, respectively, which is below the cutoff of
1.1, indicating that aprocitentan does not have the potential to inhibit OATP1B1 and OATP1B3.
OAT1 and OAT3 Imax,u/IC50 values were 0.026 and 0.083, respectively. As these values are lower
than the cutoff of 0.1, aprocitentan does not inhibit OAT. As the IC50 for OCT and MATE were
>100µM, aprocitentan does not inhibit these transporters.
Metabolite M1 is formed by hydrolysis and was formed with and without the presence of
enzymes. In human hepatocytes, 6 metabolites were found across all batches (M1-M6). M1 was
present in the highest concentrations, followed by secondary and tertiary metabolites M2, M5,
M4, and M6. The M3 glucoside metabolite was seen in a single hepatocyte batch at lower levels
than the other observed metabolites. UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 were shown to catalyze the
formation of M3.
Aprocitentan had no or minimal inhibition on most human CYP enzymes, including CYP1A2,
CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2D6, and CYP2E1. All members of the CYP2C family were inhibited,
with IC50 values ranging from 15 to 31µM. A Ki value of 11µM was determined on CYP2C9.
Aprocitentan also inhibited CYP3A4 in vitro for both the midazolam and testosterone markers,
with IC50 values of 7.3µM and 11µM, respectively. Aprocitentan did not inhibit CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, or CYP3A4 in a time-dependent manner.
Aprocitentan inhibited UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 (IC50 values of 23µM and 43µM, respectively).
For CYP3A4, assuming a conservative unbound fraction in plasma of 1% and a correction of Ki
for the fraction of unbound drug in the test system by 0.8 (in vitro tests indicated values between
0.8 and 1.0), the R1 and R1,gut values for CYP3A4 were 1.01 and 37, respectively. The R1 value
was below the recommended threshold of 1.02, but the R1,gut value was above the threshold of
11. A clinical drug-drug interaction study for CYP3A4 was conducted (Section 14.2).
Aprocitentan activated human PXR with an EC50 of 7.2 to 8.7µM. Concentration-dependent
increases in CYP3A4 mRNA and enzyme activity were observed. CYP3A4 activity was
148
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
increased by up to 3.9-fold, and CYP3A4 mRNA was increased by up to 6.7-fold at the highest
aprocitentan concentration (10µM), though no relevant changes in CYP1A2 and CYP2C9
activity were observed. Of note, the expected aprocitentan Cmax,u at steady state is about 0.07µM.

14.2. In Vivo Studies
Study AC-080-101: Single-Center, Three-Part Phase 1 Study to Investigate the Safety,
Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of ACT-132577 in Healthy Male
and Female Subjects
Study Design
This three-part Phase 1 study was composed of Part A, an open-label, single-dose, Phase 1 pilot
study; Part B, a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled parallel-group, single ascending
dose Phase 1 study (including food effect); and Part C, a double-blind, randomized, placebocontrolled, parallel-group, multiple ascending dose Phase 1 study (including healthy elderly
subjects). This study used aprocitentan in capsules.
In Part A, PK, safety, and tolerability was assessed for a single 5 mg dose. In Part B, PK was
assessed for single doses of 25, 100, 300, or 600 mg. Food effect was also assessed. In Part C,
multiple doses of aprocitentan in healthy male and female subjects were assessed. Doses studied
included 5, 25, or 100 mg administered for 10 days. PK in elderly subjects were also assessed.
Results
After a single dose, aprocitentan was more rapidly absorbed at lower doses than higher doses,
with a median Tmax of 3 hours after a single 5 mg dose compared to 9 hours after a single 600 mg
dose (Table 78). After a single dose, Cmax and AUC0-∞ were less than dose proportional (Table
78).

After multiple-dose administration, mean increases from baseline in body weight were observed

g/dL for 5, 25, and 100 mg doses, respectively. The median decrease in hemoglobin for those on
placebo was 1.1. There were also decreases in hematocrit and red blood cells.
Agency Assessment
Aprocitentan exposure increased with food and was higher in females and the elderly.
Aprocitentan is intended for chronic use and these differences in AUC are relatively minor;
therefore, no dosage adjustments are warranted for food, sex, or age. At steady sate, there were
treatment- and dose-related decreases in hemoglobin and hematocrit, and after multiple doses of
100 mg aprocitentan, body weight increased more compared to subjects receiving placebo.
Study AC-080-104: Single-Center, Open-Label Study With 14C-Radiolabeled ACT-132577
to Investigate the Mass Balance, Pharmacokinetics, and Metabolism Following Single Oral
Administration to Healthy Male Subjects
Study Design
This study was a single-center, open-label mass balance study with 14C-radiolabeled
aprocitentan. A single oral dose of aprocitentan was administered in a capsule, followed by an
observation period of 14 days where blood, urine, and feces samples were collected.
154
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

Results
The mean age of the 6 enrolled subjects was 63.0 years (range 61-64 years) with a mean body
mass index of 25.23 kg/m2 (range 22.2-27.2 kg/m2). After administration, 52.12% of the
administered dose was recovered in the urine, and 24.79% of the administered dose was
recovered in the feces. Total cumulative recovery ranged from 65.8% to 82.9%, with 5 out of 6
subjects having a total cumulative recovery of >75%.
Exposure to 14C-radioactivity was lower in whole blood compared to plasma, and the estimated
blood/plasma ratio based on Cmax and AUC0-∞ was 0.59 and 0.63, respectively. The mean Cmax
(966 ng/mL) and median Tmax (5.0 h) of aprocitentan in plasma were comparable to that of 14Cradioactivity (mean Cmax 1060 ng eq/mL; median Tmax 4.0 h) (Table 86 and Table 87).

Agency Assessment

most of the radioactivity. In urine, the main products were M3, M6, M16, and A1. In feces, the

Study AC-080-105: A Single-Center, Open-Label, Single-Dose Study to Investigate the
Effect of Severe Renal Impairment on the Pharmacokinetics of ACT-132577
Design
The primary objective was to investigate the PK of aprocitentan in those with severe renal
impairment compared to healthy subjects. Aprocitentan 50 mg was administered in a capsule,
and blood samples were obtained over 24 hours and in the mornings of Days 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, and
12 for PK analysis. 16 subjects were enrolled in two groups: Group A (8 healthy subjects), and
Group B (8 subjects with severe renal function impairment). Healthy subjects were matched to
renal impairment subjects based on age, sex, body weight, and height.
Results
PK was similar after a single oral dose of 50 mg in healthy subjects and subjects with severe
renal impairment (Table 89). Cmax was similar between groups, with a geometric mean ratio of
1.04 (90% confidence interval [CI] of 0.85 to 1.28) (Table 90). The Tmax was 5 hours and 7.6
hours in healthy subjects and those with severe renal impairment, respectively. Clearance was
lower in those with severe renal impairment and half-life was longer at 53.2 hours in those with
severe renal impairment compared to 47.4 hours in healthy subjects. AUC0-t and AUC0-∞ were
32% and 34% higher in those with severe renal impairment, respectively (Table 90). Plasma
protein binding was similar between healthy subjects and those with severe renal impairment,
ranging from 99.04% to 99.19%.

Agency Assessment
Generally, the PK of a single 50 mg dose of aprocitentan was similar between normal subjects
and those with severe renal impairment. The geometric mean ratio for Cmax was 1.04, meaning
there was no clinically relevant difference between the Cmax in those with severe renal function
and healthy subjects. The AUC0­t and AUC0­∞ were 32% and 34% higher, respectively, in those
with severe renal impairment compared to those with normal renal function. Based on the results
of this study, aprocitentan can be administered to those with severe renal impairment without the
need for dose adjustment. This also means that those with moderate and mild renal impairment
may take aprocitentan without dose adjustment.

Study ID-080-109: An Open-Label Phase 1 Study to Investigate the Effect of Moderate
Hepatic Impairment Due to Liver Cirrhosis on the Pharmacokinetics of a Single Dose of
25 mg Aprocitentan
Design
The primary objective was to investigate the PK of a single dose of 25 mg aprocitentan in
subjects with moderate hepatic impairment due to liver cirrhosis compared to healthy subjects.
Aprocitentan was administered as a tablet, and blood samples were drawn through 312 hours
postdose. 16 subjects were enrolled in two groups: Group 1 (8 subjects with moderate hepatic
impairment, Child-Pugh Grade B), and Group 2 (8 healthy subjects). Healthy subjects were
matched to subjects with moderate hepatic impairment with regard to sex, age, body weight, and
height.
Results
The PK of aprocitentan after a single oral dose of 25 mg was similar between those with
moderate hepatic impairment and healthy subjects (Table 91). Peak plasma concentrations were
reached in 4 hours in both groups. The geometric mean ratio (90% CI) for Cmax was 1.03 (0.861.24) (Table 92). Clearance was lower in those with moderate hepatic impairment, and half-life
was longer (Table 91). The AUC0-t and AUC0-∞ were 22% and 23% higher, respectively, in those
with moderate hepatic impairment compared to healthy subjects (Table 92). There were no
differences in plasma protein binding between those with moderate hepatic impairment and
healthy subjects, with mean values ranging from 98.7% to 99% (Table 91).

Study ID-080-107: A Single-Center, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Randomized Study
to Investigate the Tolerability, Safety, and Pharmacokinetics After Multiple-Dose
Administration of 25 mg Aprocitentan in Healthy Japanese and Caucasian Subjects
Design
This was a single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized Phase 1 study in healthy
Japanese and Caucasian subjects. Caucasian subjects were matched to Japanese for sex and body
weight. 25 mg aprocitentan was administered in tablets orally for 10 days in the fasted condition.
A total of 10 Japanese and 10 Caucasian healthy male and female subjects (1:1 sex ratio)
between 20 and 45 years were included in the study. The primary objective of this study was to
evaluate the relative PK characteristics of multiple-dose administration of 25 mg aprocitentan in
Japanese versus Caucasian healthy subjects. The PK analysis set included 4 male and 4 female
subjects that received active treatment (1 male and 1 female received placebo).
Results
The PK of aprocitentan after administration of 25 mg once daily for 10 days was similar in
Japanese and Caucasian subjects. Steady state was reached between Day 7 and Day 10, with a
geometric mean peak-trough fluctuation of 43.91 and 52.73% for Japanese and Caucasian
subjects, respectively (Table 93). Accumulation at steady state was similar for both groups, with
geometric mean accumulation index values of 2.93 and 3.05 for Japanese and Caucasian
subjects, respectively (Table 93). The Cmax geometric means ratio after the last dose was 0.94 for
males, 1.1 for females, and 1.0 overall (Table 94). The AUC0-∞ after the last dosing interval was
0.99 for males, 1.2 for females, and 1.1 overall (Table 94).

Study ID-080-103: A Single-Center, Open-Label, Two-Treatment Study to Investigate the
Effect of ACT-132577 at Steady State on the Pharmacokinetics of Midazolam and its
Metabolite 1-Hydroxy Midazolam in Healthy Male Subjects
Design
This study was a single-center, open-label, single-sequence, two-treatment Phase 1 study. The
primary objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan on the PK of midazolam and 1hydroxy midazolam. All subjects received a single dose of 8 mg midazolam on Day 1 in the
fasted condition, and a 24-hour PK profile was collected (Treatment A). Then, on Day 2, all
subjects received a single dose of 150 mg aprocitentan. On Days 3, 4, and 5, a trough (predose)
blood sample was collected, then 50 mg aprocitentan was administered (Treatment B1). On Day
6, a trough (predose) blood sample was collected, and a single dose of 8 mg midazolam was
administered plus a single dose of 50 mg aprocitentan, and a 24-hour PK profile was collected
(Treatment B2). Aprocitentan was administered in capsules.
Results
Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations had minimal effect on the PK of midazolam
(Table 95). The geometric mean ratios (90% Cis) for AUC0-t, AUC0-∞, and Cmax were 1.14 (1.061.23), 1.14 (1.06-1.22), and 1.04 (0.88-1.23), respectively (Table 96). Steady-state aprocitentan
plasma concentrations also had minimal effect on 1-hydroxy midazolam PK (Table 95). The
geometric mean ratios (90% Cis) for AUC0-t, AUC0-∞, and Cmax were 0.95 (0.89-1.03), 0.98
(0.91-1.05), and 0.86 (0.70-1.05) (Table 96). Additionally, half-life for 1-hydrozy midazolam
significantly increased from 5.8 to 7.7 hours (p=0.0141) (Table 95,Table 96).

Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations did not have a clinically meaningful effect on

within the 0.8 to 1.25 interval. Steady state aprocitentan plasma concentrations did not have any
effect on AUC0­t or AUC0­∞ of 1-hydroxy midazolam. However, the Cmax of 1-hdyroxy midazolam
was reduced, and the half-life increased by about 2 hours. Overall, the results of this study
indicate that aprocitentan does not alter the PK of midazolam or its metabolite to a clinically
relevant extent; therefore, aprocitentan can be administered with CYP3A4 substrates without
dose adjustment.

Study AC-080-106: A Single-Center, Open-Label, One-Sequence, Two-Treatment Study to
Investigate the Effect of ACT-132577 at Steady State on the Pharmacokinetics of
Rosuvastatin in Healthy Male Subjects
Design
This is a prospective, single-center, open-label, one-sequence, two-treatment Phase 1 study. The
primary objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan at steady state on the PK of a single
dose of rosuvastatin in healthy male subjects. Each subject received the following treatments:
Treatment A – in the morning of Day 1, a single dose of 10 mg rosuvastatin was administered in
the fasted state followed by an observation period of 96 hours; Treatment B1 – doses of 25 mg
aprocitentan were administered once daily from Day 5 to Day 12; and Treatment B2 – in the
morning of Day 13, a single dose of 10 mg rosuvastatin was administered in the fasted state
concomitantly with 25 mg aprocitentan, followed by an observation period of 120 hours. Doses
of 25 mg aprocitentan were administered once daily from Day 14 to Day 17. Aprocitentan was
administered as a capsule.
Results
Administration with aprocitentan increased the Cmax of rosuvastatin by 40% (90% CI of the
geometric means: 1.19-1.65) (Table 97, Table 98). The half-life decreased by 21% (90% CI of
the geometric means: 0.6-1.03) (Table 97, Table 98). AUC0-t and AUC0-∞ were not affected with
geometric mean ratios of 1.07 and 0.99, respectively (Table 97, Table 98). The 90% Cis for AUC
were contained within the 0.80 to 1.25 reference interval (Table 98). Mean half-life of
rosuvastatin decreased from 17.9 hours to 14.1 hours, though this change was not significant
(p=0.14) (Table 97, Table 98).

Study ID-080-108: Prospective, Single-Center, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo- and
Moxifloxacin-Controlled, 4-Way Crossover Phase 1 Study to Assess the Effect of Multiple
Therapeutic and Supratherapeutic Doses of Aprocitentan on the QT Interval Duration in
Healthy Subjects
Design
This study was a prospective, single-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo- and
moxifloxacin-controlled (open-label), 2-way crossover Phase 1 study. The primary objective was
to demonstrate, using concentration-QT interval (QT) analysis in healthy male and female
subjects, that multiple-dose administration of oral therapeutic and supratherapeutic doses of
aprocitentan do not have a clinically significant effect on the QT interval. Aprocitentan was
administered as 25 mg tablets. There were 4 periods in which subjects were to receive, in a
randomly assigned sequence, each of the following 4 treatments: Treatment A – repeated
administration of a 25 mg oral dose of aprocitentan from Day 1 to Day 10, followed by 18 days
of observation; Treatment B – repeated administration of a 100 mg oral dose or aprocitentan
from Day 1 to Day 10, followed by 18 days of observation; Treatment C – repeated
administration of an aprocitentan-matching placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 10, followed
by 18 days of observation; and Treatment D – repeated administration of an oral dose of
aprocitentan-matching placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 9, and on Day 10, oral
administration of one single 400 mg moxifloxacin tablet open-label, followed by 18 days of
observation. Each of the 4 periods were separated by a minimum of 2 days.
Results
The QT results are discussed in Section 7.6.9.2, and the PK results for aprocitentan are discussed
here. Cmax and AUCτ increased approximately dose-proportionally, and the accumulation index
was about 3 (Table 101).

Study AC-080-102: A Single-Center, Double-Blind, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled, TwoWay Crossover Study to Evaluate Potential Body Weight Increase Induced by Fluid
Retention After Administration of ACT-132577 in Healthy Male Subjects on a HighSodium Diet
Design
This was a prospective, single-center, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, two-way
crossover, Phase 1 study in healthy male subjects on a high sodium diet (HSD). The primary
objective was to evaluate the effect of aprocitentan on body weight in healthy male subjects on
an HSD. Aprocitentan was administered in capsules.
Subjects were randomized to one of three aprocitentan dose levels and placebo and to one of two
treatment sequences (A-B or B-A) in a 1:1 ratio. Treatment A received 10, 25, or 50 mg of
aprocitentan once daily from Day 1 to Day 9. Subjects followed an HSD from Day −3 to Day 9.
After the last dose of aprocitentan, subjects stopped the HSD and were observed for 10‒12 days.
Treatment B received placebo once daily from Day 1 to Day 9 Subjects were on an HSD for Day
-3 to Day 9. After the last intake of placebo, subjects stopped the HSD and were observed for 1012 days. The two treatment periods were separated by a washout of at least 7 days.
Results
After multiple dosing for 9 days, Ctrough increased roughly dose proportionally (mean trough
concentrations of 781, 1817, and 3694 ng/mL for the 10 mg, 25 mg, and 50 mg doses,
respectively).
The 10 mg aprocitentan dose was considered noninferior to placebo because the upper limit of
the 90% CI did not exceed 1 kg (mean increase of +0.43 kg, CI 0.05-0.80). The 25 mg and
50 mg doses had an upper limit of the 90% CI that exceeded 1 kg, so noninferiority was not
concluded (mean body weight increases of +0.77 kg, CI 0.03-1.51 and +0.83 kg, CI 0.33-1.32,
respectively).
A decrease in Hb was seen in those on aprocitentan compared to placebo. Placebo-corrected
differences between baseline and Day 9 were -3.1, -3.3, and -5.5 g/L for the 10 mg, 25 mg, and
50 mg doses of aprocitentan, respectively. There were slight, nondose-dependent decreases in
Hct seen, as placebo-corrected differences between baseline and Day 9 were -0.5, -1, and 0 g/L
for 10, 25, and 50 mg, respectively.
Aprocitentan did not affect sodium excretion parameters in a clear way, as sodium clearance,
FENa, and FELi (a marker of proximal tubule sodium reabsorption) were unchanged during the 10
hour collection period on Days 1 and 9. Compared with placebo, there were small differences in
sodium excretion rates during the 10 hour collection periods on Days 1 and 9. On Day 9 but not
on Day 1, there was a lower sodium excretion rate with 10 mg aprocitentan than with placebo;
however, this was not confirmed at the 25 mg dose level. Further, on Day 1 and Day 9, there was
a consistently higher sodium excretion rate with 50 mg aprocitentan than placebo (see Figure
17).

There were no clear patterns of changes in body fluid-regulating hormones between baseline and
Day 9.
There appeared to be a small, nondose-dependent increase in extracellular water (ECW) with all
three doses of aprocitentan, and there did not appear to be an increase in intracellular water.

There was no effect on systolic blood pressure (SBP), but there was a small decrease in diastolic
blood pressure (DBP) between baseline and Day 9 (placebo-corrected decrease of -3.6, -4.6, and
-4.8 mmHg for the 10, 25, and 50 mg aprocitentan doses, respectively) (Table 102).

Agency Assessment
The 10 mg dose is considered noninferior to placebo for body weight gain; however,
noninferiority cannot be confirmed for the 25 and 50 mg doses, as the 90% CI upper limit
exceeded 1 kg. While it appears that there may be some hemodilution from aprocitentan
treatment while on an HSD, there was no clear dose-response relationship. There was also a
nondose-dependent increase in ECW. Doses were generally well tolerated, but there was a
decrease in Hb and a nondose-dependent decrease in Hct. There were also small decreases in
DBP, but not SBP.
Study AC-080A201: Multi-Center, Double-Blind, Double-Dummy, Randomized, Placeboand Active-Reference, Parallel Group, Phase 2 Dose-Finding Study With ACT-132577 in
Subjects With Essential Hypertension (Grade 1 and 2)
Design
This study was a prospective, multi-center, double-blind, double-dummy, randomized, placeboand active-reference, parallel group, Phase 2, dose-finding study with aprocitentan administered
in a capsule in subjects with essential hypertension (grade 1 and 2). The primary objective was to
explore the dose response of aprocitentan on DBP in subjects with grade 1 or 2 essential
hypertension. Secondary objectives included evaluation of the dose-response of aprocitentan on
SBP, control and response rate of blood pressure, 24-hour ambulatory blood pressure monitoring
(ABPM), and safety and tolerability. Other objectives included evaluation of the steady-state
trough plasma concentrations of aprocitentan and possible metabolites in hypertensive subjects,
evaluation of steady-state trough plasma concentrations of ET-1 in hypertensive subjects, and
investigation of the correlation between ET-1 and aprocitentan plasma concentrations.
180
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
This study consisted of the following periods: prescreening, screening, run-in (Period I),
treatment (Period II), withdrawal (Period III), follow-up, and end-of-study. The run-in period
(Period I) was a single-blind period of 4 to 6 weeks. For antihypertensive treatment-naïve
subjects, Period I lasted 4 weeks, and for subjects on antihypertensive treatment, Period I lasted 6
weeks, including a 2-week washout period for antihypertensive treatment. Period II was the
double-blind treatment period of 8 weeks. Period III was the single-blind placebo period of 2
weeks where subjects were given a withdrawal placebo. 490 subjects were randomized in a
1:1:1:1:1:1 ratio into the following 6 groups: 4 aprocitentan dose groups (5, 10, 25, and 50 mg),
20 mg lisinopril, and placebo.
Results
Mean (SD) changes from baseline in mean trough sitting diastolic blood pressure (SiDBP) were 6.3 (8.9), -9.9 (8.7), -12 (8.2), and -10 (7.9) mmHg for the 5, 10, 25, and 50 mg aprocitentan
dose groups respectively, -4.9 (11.1) mmHg for placebo, and -8.4 (9.6) mmHg for 20 mg
lisinopril. Mean (SD) changes from baseline in mean trough sitting systolic blood pressure
(SiSBP) were −10.3 (15.3), −15.0 (14.5), −18.5 (15.0) and −15.1 (11.8) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25
and 50 mg aprocitentan groups, respectively, compared with −7.7 (18.8) mmHg in the placebo
group and −12.8 (16.0) mmHg in the 20 mg lisinopril group.
Mean (SD) changes from baseline to Week 8 in 24-h mean DBP were −3.76 (6.36), −6.55
(7.04), −8.86 (7.35) and −5.98 (6.63) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25 and 50 mg aprocitentan groups,
respectively, compared with −2.49 (5.52) mmHg in the placebo group. The mean change from
baseline for DBP in the 20 mg lisinopril group was −5.72 (9.12) mmHg. Mean (SD) changes
from baseline to Week 8 in 24-h mean SBP were −3.16 (10.54), −7.72 (11.37), −9.23 (9.92)
and −6.07 (8.93) mmHg in the 5, 10, 25 and 50 mg aprocitentan groups, respectively, compared
with −3.54 (7.46) mmHg in the placebo group. The mean change from baseline in the 20 mg
lisinopril group was −7.23 (14.33) mmHg.
Steady-state conditions were reached by Week 2 across all aprocitentan treatment groups,
indicated by similar trough plasma concentrations at Weeks 2, 4, and 8.
ET-1 concentrations ranged from 1.84-1.97 pg/mL for the placebo group to 2.99-3.16 pg/mL for
the 50 mg aprocitentan group.
A dose-dependent decrease in hemoglobin from baseline to Week 8 was seen for aprocitentan
groups (range from -1.3 to -6.7 g/L) compared with a +2.2 g/L increase in the placebo group.
There was no change in mean body weight for any aprocitentan dose group, which suggests that
there may not have been significant fluid retention.
Agency Assessment
Generally, change from baseline to Week 8 in mean trough SiDBP decreased dose dependently
up to 25 mg aprocitentan; however, the 50 mg dose of aprocitentan appears to have resulted in a
smaller decrease in SiDBP than the 25 mg dose (-10 mmHg versus -12 mmHg, respectively).
This trend was consistent with SiSBP results. Given that both the 10 mg and 50 mg aprocitentan
doses appeared to decrease blood pressure to a similar extent, and the 25 mg dose appeared to
have the greatest effect on blood pressure in this study, doses at or near 10 mg and 25 mg
181
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
seemed to be reasonable to explore in future studies. Two doses, a 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose, were
selected for further study in the Phase 3 study (PRECISION).
Interestingly, the arithmetic mean Ctrough values were lower (range of 1.57-1.85 μg/mL at the
different visits for the 25 mg dose; SD range of 1.27–1.43 μg/mL), with greater variability based
on SD than for observations in healthy subjects (mean Ctrough range of 2.26–3.22 μg/mL and SD
range of 0.22–0.78 μg/mL for the 25 mg dose in healthy subjects, including Japanese subjects or
subjects on a high sodium diet). There does not appear to be an explanation for this difference
between subjects in this study and healthy subjects. Of note, in the Phase 3 study (PRECISION)
in subjects with difficult-to-control hypertension, the mean Ctrough (SD) values measured at Week
4 were 1.31 (0.53) μg/mL in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg group and 2.70 (1.16) μg/mL in the
aprocitentan 25 mg group. These Ctrough concentrations were in the range of those observed in
healthy subjects at similar/equivalent doses; therefore, it is unlikely that disease state affected
the Ctrough of aprocitentan.
PRECISION: Multi-Center, Blinded, Randomized, Parallel-Group, Phase 3 Study With
Aprocitentan in Subjects With Resistant Hypertension
Design
Refer to Section 6.2.1.1 for details on the study design.
Results
Trough plasma concentrations of aprocitentan were measured at the end of Part 1. Median
plasma trough concentrations were 1310 ng/mL (range: 111–3090 ng/mL; CV%: 40.4) in the
aprocitentan 12.5 mg group and 2620 ng/mL (range: 39.1–8700 ng/mL; CV%: 43.1) in the
aprocitentan 25 mg group.
Changes in blood pressure from baseline to Week 4 and from baseline of the double-blind (DB)withdrawal (WD) to Week 40 for the primary and secondary endpoints are summarized in Table
103. Decreases in systolic blood pressure were similar for the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose between
baseline and Week 4. During the DB-WD, SiSBP and SiDBP continued to decrease slightly in
those on 25 mg aprocitentan and increase in those on placebo.
Changes in 24 hour mean SBP and DBP from baseline to Week 4 and from the DB-WD baseline
to Week 40 are in Table 104. From baseline to Week 4, the 25 mg dose of aprocitentan decreased
the mean 24 hour SBP and DBP more than the 12.5 mg dose. In the DB-WD period, those given
placebo had increases in both 24 hour mean SBP and DBP. Changes in mean daytime and
nighttime SBP and DBP from baseline to Week 4 and from the DB-WD baseline to Week 40 are
in Table 105. From baseline to Week 4, the 25 mg dose of aprocitentan decreased the mean
daytime and nighttime SBP and DBP more than the 12.5 mg dose. During the DB-WD period,
the daytime and nighttime mean SBP and DBP increased for those on placebo. While the mean
nighttime SBP increased by 0.26 mmHg between the DB-WD and Week 40, likely due to
variability, the increase for those on placebo was much higher (mean increase of 8.76 mmHg).

A subgroup analysis was conducted for African American or Black subjects (Table 106) and
those with an eGFR >60 mL/min/1.73 m2 (Table 107). A subgroup analysis was also conducted
for those with chronic kidney disease (CKD) stage 3-4 and those aged 75 years and older (see
section below on Assessment of Dose and Potential effectiveness). In African American or Black
subjects, during the 4-week double-blind treatment, when corrected for placebo, SiSBP measured
by uAOPBM increased in those assigned to either the 12.5 mg or 25 mg dose when corrected for
placebo; however, SiDBP decreased. All ABPM measurements (24 hour mean, daytime mean,
and nighttime mean) decreased compared to placebo in subjects with eGFR >60 mL/min/1.73
m2, during the 4-week double-blind treatment, the SiSBP and SiDBP measured by uAOPBM
decreased compared to placebo, with a greater mean decrease in those assigned to the 12.5 mg
dose rather than the 25 mg dose; however, ABPM measurements (24 hour, daytime, and
nighttime mean) all decreased more with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg dose.

Agency Assessment
In the full population, the 12.5 mg and 25 mg dose showed similar SiSBP and SiDBP lowering in
the first 4 weeks in the DB treatment period. ABPM parameters showed a mean greater
improvement with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg dose. The extent of the benefit of the
12.5 mg and 25 mg dose varied in different populations (i.e., different renal function status and
race). In the DB-WD period, those remaining on 25 mg aprocitentan maintained lower mean
blood pressure measurements compared to those who switched to placebo.

with stage 3-4 chronic kidney disease (Table 111), the population without Black or African

endpoint was seen in those with stage 3 and 4 CKD. Subgroup analyses by baseline CKD stage
showed that the heterogeneity observed for CKD stages 1-2 compared to CKD stages 3-4 for the
25 mg dose was likely due to random chance (refer to subgroup analyses in Section 6.2.1.4 for
details). Additionally, per the Applicant, there was a larger decrease in the primary blood
pressure endpoint in the >75-year-old population with the 25 mg dose compared to the 12.5 mg
dose.

In a further attempt to determine if there is any additional benefit with the 25 mg dose compared
to the 12.5 mg dose, the change in blood pressure endpoints from Week 4 (end of Part 1) to
Week 36 (end of Part 2) for those randomized to 12.5 mg in Part 1 was assessed (Table 114).
The data at a single timepoint for the change in SiSBP for each subject from Week 4 to Week 36
is plotted in Figure 18. The data in Figure 18 shows that while many subjects appeared to have
derived further benefit with a decrease in SiSBP from an increased aprocitentan dose, many
other subjects did not.
Because SiSBP readings in the office can be variable, 24 hour mean SBP measured by ABPM
may be a better measure of change in systolic blood pressure. The data for the change in 24 hour
mean SBP measured by ABPM between Week 4 and Week 36 is presented in Figure 19. Similar
to SiSBP, there were many subjects that had a decrease in 24 hour mean SBP measured by
ABPM from an increased aprocitentan dose, though many others did not. The mean difference in
24 hour SBP measured by ABPM was -0.5 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 19, there are a few
individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure.

To evaluate if the duration of treatment alone may have an impact on BP, the change in blood
pressure endpoints from Week 4 (end of Part 1) to Week 36 (end of Part 2) for those randomized
to 25 mg in Part 1 (and remained on 25 mg dose in Part 2) was also assessed (Table 115). The
data for the change in SiSBP for each individual from Week 4 to Week 36 is plotted in Figure
20. Similar to the data in Figure 18, the data in Figure 20 shows that while many subjects had a
decrease in SiSBP from maintaining a 25 mg aprocitentan dose, many other subjects did not. The
mean difference in SiSBP was -0.84 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 20, there are several
individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure. Therefore, it does not
appear that there was an additional large decrease in blood pressure from Week 4 to Week 36 in
those receiving a 25 mg aprocitentan dose. The decrease that is seen in SiSBP could be due to
variability. Similar to SiSBP, there were many subjects that had decreased 24 hour mean SBP
measured by ABPM when remaining on a 25 mg dose, though many others did not. The mean
difference in 24-hour SBP measured by ABPM was 1.32 mmHg, and as seen in Figure 21, there
are a few individuals who appear to have much larger changes in blood pressure.

Some blood pressure endpoints (SiSBP and SiDBP) were also assessed at Week 48 after the
double-blind withdrawal period and were compared to Week 4 values (Table 116 and Table
117). There appears to be a greater reduction in BP in subjects who changed dose from 12.5 mg
to 25 mg compared to those who stayed on the 25 mg dose; however, the differences were small.

for those who remained on 25 mg aprocitentan.

14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Performance
Bioanalytical Method for Aprocitentan
Bioanalytical method METH-051 was developed for detecting aprocitentan in plasma via liquid
chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry. A full method validation was completed, then
in-study validation was conducted for each clinical study. Bioanalytical lifecycle information is
included in Table 118, and a summary of the method and method performance is in Table 119.

Bioanalytical Method for 14C Radioactivity
A method for determining 14C radioactivity in human plasma by liquid scintillation counting was
(b) (4)
(b) (4)
developed and validated by
-024033/
-RD-559).
The method had a 3-run validation, and the method was considered validated if the within-run,
between-run, and overall precision were <15% (20% at the LLQ), and if the bias was between 15% and 15% (between -20% and 20% at the LLQ). The within-run, between-run, and overall
coefficients of variation were not higher than 19% at LLQ and not higher than 5% at other QC
levels. The overall bias of the heparin plasma method for the 1.00, 0.500, and 0.250 mL assay
was between 5.2% and 16.3%. For hemolytic heparin plasma, the accuracy and precision results
met the criteria except for the hemolytic samples at the LLQ, which showed an overall CV%
>20% for the 1.00 mL assay and a bias >20% for the 0.250 mL assay. It was noted that
hemolysis does not normally occur during plasma sample preparation, but if it were to occur, a
high CV% or high bias is only observed at the LLQ, indicating that hemolysis generally has a
low impact on the study results. The study also showed that both heparin and EDTA could be
used as an anticoagulant, as the results for experiments measuring the effect of EDTA plasma
samples did not deviate by more than 15% from the mean activity in heparin plasma samples for
all 5 activity levels. One deviation occurred: for plasma, the quench curve used for efficiency
corrections was not a 14C low level quenched standard set, but an in-house prepared quench
curve was made. It was noted that the use of the in-house prepared quench curve significantly

improved the assay method. In an amendment (

14.5.2. Population PK Analysis
14.5.2.1. Review Summary
The Applicant’s PopPK analysis for aprocitentan is acceptable to support the current submission.
The Applicant’s final PopPK model adequately described the aprocitentan concentrations, in
which PopPK parameter estimates were estimated with acceptable precision as indicated by the
relative standard errors (RSE) for total body clearance of drug (CL, 1.9%, RSE), volume of
distribution in central compartment (Vc, 0.4% RSE), volume of distribution in peripheral
compartment (Vp, 2.4% RSE), intercompartment clearance (Q, 16.6% RSE) and Ka (7.3% RSE).
The goodness-of-fit plots showed good agreement between the observed and the individual
predicted concentrations without any obvious bias over time. The prediction-corrected visual
predictive check plots showed good agreement between the observed and the simulated
concentrations. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant
discordance identified. For further comments on the utility of the model to support labeling and
derive exposure metrics for exposure-response analyses, see Table 125.

14.5.2.2. Introduction
The primary objectives of the Applicant’s PopPK analysis were to:
•

Develop a population PK model describing aprocitentan plasma concentration over time at
the population level and at the individual level.

•

Investigate the relationship between covariates and model parameters.

•

Quantify and visualize the influence of identified covariates on the concentration-time profile
of aprocitentan via model-based simulations, enabling judgment about the clinical relevance
of the covariates.
14.5.2.3. PopPK Model Development

Data
A total of 902 subjects receiving active treatment were included in the PK modeling data set, of
which 251 subjects were enrolled in Phase 1 studies, 226 were enrolled in a Phase 2 study
conducted in subjects with essential hypertension, and 425 were enrolled in a Phase 3 study
conducted in subjects with resistant hypertension. For an overview of the studies, see Table 126.
A total of 356 out of 8450 (4.2%) aprocitentan concentration measurements in the modeling
dataset were below the limit of quantification; most of these measurements were predose
measurements obtained in Phase 1 studies.12 Measurements below the limit of quantification
were treated by M4 method, which was set to the LLOQ of 5 ng/mL and treated as censored in
the PopPK analysis. Predose PK measurements obtained prior to first study treatment
administration were set to a value of 0.05 ng/mL (censored) and specified as a missing dependent
variable in the modeling data set (i.e., not used for modeling).
In addition to the 8450 (94.4%) records, a total of 498 records (5.6%) were kept in the data set
but flagged for exclusion for various reasons, such as extreme low values, missed dosing time,
missed sampling time, etc. 13
Agency Assessment
The Applicant excluded observations with extremely low values, which may affect parameter
estimates of PopPK models. Model parameter estimates based on the inclusion of extremely low
values and creatinine imputation were similar to those of the Applicant’s final PopPK model.
Therefore, the Applicant’s final PopPK model is considered acceptable.

Software and Estimation Methods
Population PK modeling was performed using nonlinear mixed effects modeling techniques
implemented in Monolix version 2021R1 (Lixoft 2021a). The stochastic approximation
expectation-maximization (SAEM) algorithm was used for parameter estimation (Delyon et al.
1999). Exploratory graphics were created in R version 4.04. Simulations were performed with
Simulx version 2021R1 (Lixoft 2021b) and Berkeley Madonna (Marcoline et al. 2022).
14.5.2.4. Final Model
The parameter estimates for the final PopPK model are listed in Table 129. The goodness-of-fit
plots for the final model are shown in Figure 24. The prediction-corrected visual predictive
check plot for the final covariate model with all data is shown in Figure 25 (Stratified by dose),
and estimate of interindividual variability and conditional weighted residual histograms for the
final PopPK model are shown in Figure 26.
The final PopPK model was a 2-compartmental model as parameterized with Tlag, CL, V2, V3,
Q. An exponential model was used for interindividual variability, and a combined additive and
proportional error model was used for intra-individual variability.
•

Relative bioavailability (F) was 21.7% and 43.6% lower after administration of 300 and
600 mg, respectively, compared to doses of up to 100 mg.

•

Food status was included as a covariate for absorption parameters tlag and ka.

•

Clearance and volume parameters were found to increase with body weight, and clearance
increased with higher eGFR, decreased with moderate hepatic impairment (HI), and was
slightly higher in male subjects compared to female subjects.

•

Aprocitentan clearance for a reference female subject versus a male reference subject with
body weight of 87 kg (median in the modeling data set), no HI, and eGFR of 90 mL/min/1.73
m2 was 0.342 versus 0.354 L/h, respectively.

Agency Assessment
The Applicant’s simulation did not consider intersubject variability, which is required to predict
individual exposures. Therefore, the reviewer independently simulated individual exposures with
intersubject variability, which is used to assess exposures of patients with renal impairment,
patients with hepatic impairment, and formulations. AUCss was calculated based on F1, CL and
dose, in which doses (300 mg and 600 mg) were excluded for their lower F1 values compared to
doses ≤100 mg. In order to summarize the AUCss under different groups, the AUCss was
normalized by AUCss/dose. The normalized AUCss ratios among eGFR, shown in Table 133, are
similar to those obtained via the Applicant’s evaluation, in which eGFR (90 mL/min/1.73 m2) was
treated as a reference. The normalized AUCss ratio between moderate hepatic impairment and
healthy subjects, shown in Table 134, is a little higher than that of the Applicant’s simulation (130
versus 122). The normalized AUCss ratios among formulations (capsule, tablet A, tablet B) are
shown in Table 135. As shown in the table, normalized AUCss of the tablet A and tablet B have
similar geometric means, while the normalized AUCss of the capsule has a lower geometric mean.

15.3. Study Administrative Structure
Independent Data Monitoring Committee
The independent data monitoring committee (IDMC) was composed of 4 external experts with
experience and expertise in the cardiovascular domain, statistical methodologies, safety
monitoring, and overseeing randomized clinical studies. The IDMC was fully operational prior to
enrollment of the first subject in the study. The IDMC had overall responsibility for safeguarding
the interests of subjects by monitoring unblinded safety and efficacy data obtained in the study
and making appropriate recommendations based on the reported data, thus ensuring that the
study was conducted to the highest scientific and ethical standards. Unblinded data were
delivered to the IDMC by an Independent Statistical Analysis Center. The details of the IDMC
organization, roles, and responsibilities, and conduct of meetings were defined in the IDMC
charter.
Each meeting begins with an open session that Applicant representatives and coordinating
investigators could attend. Data presented in the open session could include recruitment status,
clinical study conduct (e.g., frequency of protocol deviations, withdrawal of consent from study,
lost to follow-up, subject discontinuation, list of serious adverse events [AEs]), protocol
amendments, communications with Health Authorities and any other metrics if needed. This was
followed by a closed session, in which the IDMC discussed the unblinded results. A member of
the Independent Statistical Analysis Center attended the closed session meeting (e.g., to present
results and answer questions).
IDMC met nine times over the course of the study and conducted nine unblinded data reviews.
Following evaluation of subject data during the third IDMC meeting (February 3, 2020), the
IDMC recommended that subjects receiving treatment with high doses of loop diuretics be
excluded from participation in the study. A letter was sent to the investigators on
February 19, 2020 to exclude subjects with high doses of loop diuretics and exclusion criterion
#11 was amended accordingly (Global Protocol Amendment 2).
See Section 15.5 for information on the administrative structure as relates to preparing the NDA
submission and/or blinding related issues.
Independent Central Adjudication Committee
The CAC was composed of 3 external experts with experience in cardiovascular / renal medicine
and expertise in the assessment of clinical events within a study. The CAC members were
responsible for adjudicating, in a standardized and blinded fashion, all AEs/serious AEs
identified by the Investigator or Sponsor which indicated potential major adverse cardiovascular
events (MACE)-plus. MACE was defined as cardiovascular death, nonfatal myocardial
infarction, or nonfatal stroke. MACE-plus included, in addition, hospitalization for heart failure.
The details of the CAC organization, roles and responsibilities, and adjudication process were
described in the CAC charter.

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)

15.4. Study Assessments
Baseline Values
All baseline blood pressure and laboratory measurements were collected during the run-in period
(Day -1). Baseline is defined as the last available measurement before or on the day of
randomization. DB-WD baseline (Week 36) is defined as the last available measurement before
or on the day of rerandomization. If both date and time of the measurement are collected,
baseline is the last measurement up to the date and time of randomization. If only the date is
collected, baseline is the last measurement before the date of randomization.
uAOBPM
An automatic oscillometric sphygmomanometer was provided to each site by the vendor and had
to be managed by qualified personnel. Wherever possible, the same person used the device for a
given subject at each visit. Sitting BP, standing BP, and pulse rate were measured noninvasively
throughout the study using this device, as described below.
BP was required to be measured at trough (i.e., before taking study treatment and standard
background antihypertensive therapy [SBAT]), at around the same clock time, at each visit.
Measurements were to be recorded from the same arm of the subject each time, using the
appropriate cuff size as determined during screening.
Details on the blood pressure procedure, including the device to be used, subject preparation
(e.g., arm selection, arm position, cuff size) were detailed in the BP laboratory manual and
followed the American Heart Association guidelines/Canadian Education Program on
Hypertension (Pickering et al. 2005; Daskalopoulou et al. 2012). Automated office blood
pressure measurement data was electronically transferred to the central BP laboratory and
subsequently to the Applicant.
Sitting BP: The uAOBPM device recorded 5 BP readings (SiSBP and SiDBP), with preset time
intervals between them. The first reading was taken after the subject had been sitting undisturbed
in a quiet place for 5 minutes; this reading was excluded, and the device generated an average
(mean trough) of the remaining four readings, taken at 1 minute intervals, for each variable.
Standing BP: monitoring of orthostatic hypotension was performed by measuring SBP and DBP
twice, approximately 1 and 3 minutes after standing.
Pulse rate: measured at each visit at the same time as BP, while sitting. The last value recorded
was used for analysis.

235
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
ABPM
ABPM devices were provided to each site by the central vendor. Subjects were required to attend
the site on 2 consecutive days for each of the following visits:
•

Baseline – End of the placebo run-in (RI) period; Visit 4 / Day –1

•

End of DB Part 1 – Visit 6 / Week 4 (Day 28±2 d)

•

End of single-blind (SB) Part 2 – Visit 11 / Week 36 (Day 252±12 d)

•

During DB-WD Part 3 – Visit 13 / Week 40 (Day 280±4 d)

On the first day, after all visit assessments had been performed, the ABPM device was applied to
the subject who then took the study treatment and SBAT in the presence of study personnel (i.e.,
direct observed treatment). The following day, the subject returned to the site to have the ABPM
device removed. The ABPM device was automatically programmed to inflate every 20 minutes
during the day (06:00–21:59) and every 30 minutes during the night (22:00–05:59), resulting in
64 readings over a 24 h period (48 during the day, 16 during the night). The main evaluation of
ABPM data was performed based on recordings with at least 70% valid readings and a total
duration of at least 21 h.
Home BP Measurement
HBPM devices were provided to each site and could be lent to subjects to monitor their own BP
at home throughout the study, as per the Investigator’s judgment. Subjects who were given an
HBPM device were instructed on its use; and informed that they had to measure their BP in the
event of any severe symptoms related to hypertension (headache, vomiting, or nosebleeds) or
orthostatic hypotension (light-headedness, presyncope, or dizziness).
Subjects were required to record their data (SBP/DBP values, time and date of measurement, and
time of study treatment intake) on the card provided with the HBPM device, for 3 days in a row.
If symptoms were still present on the third day, subjects were instructed to contact the study site.
Data recorded by subjects at home were filed in hospital charts only, not in the electronic case
report form (eCRF) and were not provided to the Applicant.
During the COVID-19 pandemic, if subjects were not allowed or not willing to travel to the
investigator site, remote visits (i.e., phone/video calls or home visits) were conducted. Subjects
were requested to measure their BP on the day before the scheduled remote visit, using either the
HBPM device provided to them for the study or their own BP device. As described above,
subjects were required to record their BP data on the HBPM card, or in a notebook (source
document), and then to provide these values to the study site staff during the remote visit. The
data were recorded in the subject’s medical chart, verified against the source at the next on-site
visit, and entered in the eCRF.

Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
Schedule of Assessments
Screening
The screening period lasted up to 12 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained:
demographics/medical history, physical examination, concomitant medications, height/weight,
12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG), hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments (see table
below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for urine albumin-to-creatinine ratio [UACR]), and
uAOBPM.
Run-In
The run-in period lasted 4 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained: physical
examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry
laboratory assessments (see table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR),
urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM.
DB Treatment
The DB treatment period lasted for 4 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained:
physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and
chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy
test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM (Week 4).
SB Treatment
The SB treatment period lasted for 32 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained:
physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and
chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy
test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM (Week
36). During the SB aprocitentan part, urine pregnancy tests were performed monthly (i.e.,
between scheduled visits) at home with kits provided to the subject.
DB-WD Treatment
The DB-WD treatment period lasted for 12 weeks, and the following assessments were obtained:
physical examination, concomitant medications, weight, 12-lead ECG, hematology and
chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see table below for details), pregnancy
test, urinalysis (for UACR), urinalysis (to monitor adherence), uAOBPM, and ABPM
(Week 40).
Follow-Up
Following completion of the treatment period, subjects entered the 30-day follow-up period
where study medication will be discontinued, except for SBAT. The following assessments were
obtained during the follow-up period: physical examination, concomitant medications, weight,
12-lead ECG, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessments including NT-proBNP (see
table below for details), pregnancy test, urinalysis (for UACR), and uAOBPM.

15.5. Study Procedures
Randomization
Randomization was performed through an interactive response technology (IRT). For each part
of the study, the investigator/delegate contacted the IRT system for allocation of subject
numbers, treatment bottle numbers, and subject randomization/rerandomization numbers, as
described below.
The IRT system was handled by an independent vendor which generated two randomization lists,
one for DB Part 1 and one for DB-WD Part 3. At Visit 1 (Screening), after a subject had signed
their ICF, the IRT system allocated a 7-digit subject number, which differed from the 5-digit
randomization number. For subjects who were rescreened, a new subject number was assigned.

238
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
Screening
During the screening period, a subject’s individual background antihypertensive medication
(except beta-blockers, if present) was standardized by switching to SBAT, which was assigned
via the IRT system. At the first run-in visit, the IRT system assigned the treatment bottle number
for the 4-week placebo RI period.
DB Treatment
At Visit 4 / Randomization (Day 1), the IRT system randomized the subject to aprocitentan
12.5 mg, aprocitentan 25 mg, or placebo, in a 1:1:1 ratio. The IRT assigned a randomization
number to the subject and a bottle number which matched the treatment arm as assigned on the
randomization list.
SB Treatment
At the end of DB Part 1 (Week 4), the IRT system assigned the first treatment bottle numbers for
the 32-week SB Part 2 (25 mg). At each subsequent visit when single-blind treatment was
dispensed, the IRT assigned the treatment bottle numbers.
DB-WD Treatment
At the end of SB Part 2 (Week 36), the IRT system rerandomized the subject to aprocitentan
25 mg or placebo in a 1:1 ratio. Rerandomization was stratified according to randomized
treatment in DB Part 1 (which was not revealed). The IRT system assigned a scrambled,
rerandomization number to the subject and bottle numbers which matched the treatment arm as
assigned on the rerandomization list. At each subsequent visit when DB-WD treatment was
dispensed, the IRT assigned the treatment bottle numbers according to the assigned treatment
arm.
Blinding and Unblinding
Blinding
Two parts of the study were performed in a single-blind fashion: the placebo RI period prior to
randomization, and SB Part 2. Study personnel were not permitted to inform subjects of the
treatment received. Two parts were performed in a double-blind fashion: DB Part 1 and DB-WD
Part 3. The Investigator and study personnel, subjects, CRAs, Sponsor personnel, and
vendor/clinical research organization (CRO) personnel involved in the conduct of the study
remained blinded to the treatment until database lock. Until the time of unblinding for final data
analysis, the randomization/rerandomization lists were kept strictly confidential and were
accessible only to Sponsor-authorized and CRO personnel who were not involved in the conduct
of the study: IRT vendor personnel (responsible for clinical study supply distribution; subject
level); the Sponsor’s Pharmaceutical Development department personnel (clinical study supply
distribution; depot level); the Sponsor’s bioanalytical and biomarker laboratories; and the
Independent Statistical Analysis Center and IDMC.

239
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
The Sponsor notes that the study treatment was provided as tablets and that the investigational
treatment and its matching placebo were indistinguishable, and all treatment bottles were
packaged in the same way.
Unblinding
Final analyses: Randomization information was made available for data analysis only after
database closure.
IDMC review: The Independent Statistical Analysis Center delivered unblinded data summaries
to the IDMC for their review.
Serious, unexpected, and suspected adverse reaction (SUSAR): When a SUSAR occurred for a
subject participating in the study, the Sponsor’s Global Drug Safety department requested the
unblinding of the treatment assignment. The treatment assignment was not communicated to site
personnel, subjects, other Sponsor personnel, or any vendor/CRO personnel involved in the
conduct of the study. Unblinded SUSAR information was provided to respective health
authorities, in addition to Independent Ethics Committees/Institutional Review Boards (as per
each committee’s/board’s specific requirements). SUSARs were reported to investigators in a
blinded fashion.
Emergency: Emergency unblinding did not occur during this study.
Dosing
For Part 1 of the study, dosages of 12.5 and 25 mg once daily were administered. For Parts 2 and
3, the aprocitentan dose administered was 25 mg once daily. Study treatment dose adjustments
were not permitted. Study treatment could be temporarily interrupted in response to an AE, or
other reasons (e.g., diagnostic or therapeutic procedure, a laboratory abnormality, study
treatment forgotten). Interruptions of study treatment were no longer than 7 consecutive days
during DB part and DB-WD part.
The decision to prematurely discontinue study treatment could be made by the subject, the
Investigator, or Sponsor personnel. The main reason (e.g., AE, hypertension grade 3) was
documented in the eCRF. Study-specific criteria for interruption / premature discontinuation of
study treatment included hypertension (mean SiSBP ≥180 mmHg and/or mean SiDBP ≥110
mmHg measured by automated office blood pressure measurement and confirmed by repeat
measurement), renal failure (confirmed eGFR <15mL/min/1.73m2 or confirmed decrease of ≥
30% from baseline), persistent fluid retention (symptoms of fluid retention are not tolerable after
increasing the dose of the existing diuretic or the addition of a new diuretic), hemoglobin
abnormalities (interruption for hemoglobin decrease from baseline of >20 g/L; discontinuation
for decrease in hemoglobin to <80 g/L, a decrease in hemoglobin from baseline of >50 g/L, need
for transfusion), pregnancy, and liver aminotransferases abnormalities (interruption for
aminotransferases ≥3 and ≤8 × upper limit of normal (ULN); discontinuation for
aminotransferases >8 × ULN, aminotransferases ≥3 × ULN and associated clinical symptoms of
liver injury, or aminotransferases ≥3 × ULN and associated increase in total bilirubin ≥2 ×
ULN).

240
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
Compliance
The study inclusion criteria include an adherence provision for randomization. Subjects could be
enrolled if they demonstrated ≥80% compliance (pill counting) to study treatment (i.e., placebo)
as well as ≥80% compliance (pill counting) to the standardized background antihypertensive
therapy during the RI period. Adherence was defined as number of tablets dispensed at visit (n)
minus number of tablets returned at Visit (n +1) divided by the number of tablets that should
have been taken during the period × 100.
Between visits, adherence based on study treatment and the standardized background
antihypertensive therapy accountability is expected to be at least 80%. Adherence values below
80% for the study treatment and/or the standardized background antihypertensive therapy
without a medical justification (e.g., AE) after the randomization visit was considered a protocol
deviation. In such cases, the Investigator was instructed to discuss and clarify the reasons for
noncompliance with the subject and take appropriate actions to avoid re-occurrence. This
discussion was to take place prior to providing the next study treatment to the subject.
Urine samples was collected throughout the study from initiation of the standardized background
antihypertensive therapy and was analyzed for the presence of valsartan (one of the components
of SBAT). The results of urine analysis from each visit were used to discuss the importance of
study treatment intake with poorly adherent subjects.
Concomitant Medications
Treatments considered necessary for the subject’s well-being and not categorized as forbidden
concomitant medications were allowed during the study and must be documented in the medical
charts.
The following therapies were allowed with the provision that they had been initiated and the
dose stable at least 4 weeks prior to the screening visit and that the dose kept stable until the
EOT: beta blockers, hormonal contraceptives, estrogen-replacement treatment, low dose
acetylsalicylic acid for prevention of cardiovascular disease. In addition, the following therapies
were allowed with the provision that they had been initiated at least 4 weeks prior to the
screening visit and that the dose was kept stable until the end of DB part: nonsteroidal antiinflammatory drugs, selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors and anxiolytics.
Antihypertensive rescue medication was allowed in Part 2 and Part 3 according to local medical
practice if, in the Investigator’s opinion, the BP was not sufficiently controlled. Uncontrolled BP
was confirmed via second measurement either at the next planned visit or at an unscheduled visit
at the Investigator’s discretion. In addition, adherence to the standardized background
antihypertensive therapy was confirmed prior to initiating additional antihypertensive
medication. Antihypertensive rescue medication was not allowed in the DB Part 1 of the study.
After Randomization, the dose of the existing diuretic (hydrochlorothiazide) could be increased,
or a new diuretic could be added in the event of fluid retention as judged by the Investigator to
be clinically relevant. The fluid retention was to be reported as an AE in the eCRF and fluid
retention was to be reported as the indication for the diuretic. If the symptom(s) of fluid retention
were not tolerable the subject was to discontinue the study treatment.
241
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5347932

NDA 217686
TRYVIO (aprocitentan)
The following concomitant therapies were forbidden from the screening visit until the EOT visit
(any drug which may affect BP, except rescue and allowed medications described above), ERAs,
or other investigational drugs.
Adverse Events of Special Interest
Adverse events of special interest were categorized as: hepatic disorders, anemia, edema / fluid
retention, and decompensation / aggravation of heart failure.
Measures to Prevent Missing Data
A subject who prematurely discontinues study treatment was not considered as withdrawn from
the study, provided that the subject’s consent for this limited participation in the study had not
been withdrawn. If a randomized subject discontinued study treatment, the subject was asked to
perform the EOT visit (i.e., Visit 15) as soon as possible after the last dose of study treatment
but, in any case, no later than 7 days and a safety FU visit 30 (+3) days thereafter. The main
reason (e.g., AE, hypertension grade 3) was documented in the eCRF. Antihypertensive
medication(s) were administered according to local standard-of-care at the Investigator’s
discretion. The standardized background antihypertensive therapy could be continued until the
completion of the safety FU visit.
A subject who prematurely discontinued study treatment at any stage and withdrew consent to
participate in any further study assessments was considered as withdrawn from the study.
Subjects who died or were lost to follow-up were also considered as withdrawn from the study.
Subjects were considered as lost to follow-up if all reasonable attempts by the Investigator to
communicate with the individual had failed. The site was instructed to take preventive measures
to avoid a subject being lost to follow-up (e.g., document different ways of contact such as
telephone number, home address, email address, person to be contacted in the event the subject
cannot be reached). If the subject could not be reached, the site was to make a reasonable effort
to contact the subject, document all attempts (date, time and type of contact made), and enter the
loss of follow-up information into the eCRF. The following methods were to be used: at least
three attempts at contacts (e.g., telephone calls, or e-mails) must be placed to the last available
telephone number or e-mail address, and one registered letter must be sent by post to the last
available home address. Additional methods were considered acceptable if they were compliant
with local rules/regulations (e.g., a visit by site personnel to the subject’s home), respecting the
subject’s right to privacy. If the subject was still unreachable after all contact attempts listed
above, he/she was considered to be lost to follow-up. The reason for premature withdrawal from
the study was recorded in the eCRF.
If for whatever reason (except death or loss-to-follow-up) a subject was withdrawn from the
study, the Investigator made his/her best efforts to schedule a last appointment / telephone call to
assess the safety and well-being of the subject, collect unused study treatment and discuss
follow-up medical care.
