Table 140. Subject Narratives for Treatment-Emergent Deaths Adjudicated as MACE, Safety Population, PRECISION Study
Subject ID
Narrative
(b) (6)
The 59-year-old White male experienced sudden death on Study Day 57. The subject began the study in the aprocitentan
12.5 mg arm and completed Part 1, switching to aprocitentan 25 mg for Part 2 on Study Day 33. On the morning of Day
57, the subject fainted, wheezed, and died. His wife called the ambulance, and emergency responders confirmed his
death upon arrival. No diagnostic or treatment procedures were performed. Prior to the event, the subject’s condition was
reported as good, with no symptoms of fluid retention, and the subject’s blood pressure was reported as 112/66 mmHg at
the last visit on Day 46. Relevant medical conditions were reported as hypertension (9 years), previous cerebrovascular
accident (7 years prior), myocardial ischemia, angina pectoris, chronic cardiac failure, supraventricular extrasystoles, type
2 diabetes mellitus, dyslipidemia, carotid arteriosclerosis, cerebral ischemia, and chronic pancreatitis. At baseline, he had
a BMI of 30.5 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 60 mL/min/1.73 m2. Concomitant medications at baseline included: acetylsalicylic
acid (100 mg daily), metformin (1000 mg twice daily), insulin human (30 IU twice daily), and atorvastatin (40 mg daily).
(b) (6)
The 68-year-old White male experienced death on Study Day 223. The subject began the study in the aprocitentan
12.5 mg arm and completed Part 1, switching to aprocitentan 25 mg for Part 2 on Study Day 29. On Day 253, the subject
did not report to the scheduled visit and the site called to follow up. The wife stated the subject died during his sleep on
Day 223. No autopsy was conducted. Prior to the event, the subject’s blood pressure was reported as 145/62 mmHg at
the last visit on Day 190. Relevant medical conditions were reported as hypertension (35 years), diabetes mellitus,
dyslipidemia, hepatic steatosis, and sleep apnea syndrome. At baseline, he had a BMI of 35.7 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 87
mL/min/1.73 m2. Concomitant medications at baseline included: acetylsalicylic acid (100 mg daily.), metformin (850 mg
three times daily), insulin human (dose unspecified), dapagliflozin (10 mg daily), dulaglutide (1.5 mg sq weekly),
fenofibrate (200 mg daily) and atorvastatin (40 mg daily).
(b) (6)
The 64-year-old White female experienced infiltrative carcinoma in the left breast on Study Day 129 which led to the
subject’s death on Study Day 213. The subject began the study in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg arm and completed Part 1,
switching to aprocitentan 25 mg for Part 2 on Study Day 31. The subject was diagnosed with breast cancer and began
chemotherapy until Study Day 213 when she was hospitalized for an unspecified reason and died the same day. The
primary cause of death was reported as invasive breast carcinoma. Autopsy revealed cerebral edema and subdural
hemorrhage at the time of death. Relevant ongoing medical conditions at Screening included cardiac failure, coronary
artery disease, myocardial fibrosis, breast neoplasm, hypercholesterolemia, cholecystitis chronic, pancreatitis chronic, and
vascular encephalopathy. At baseline, she had a BMI of 34.2 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 60 mL/min/1.73 m2. Concomitant
medications at baseline included: acetylsalicylic acid (100 mg daily) and rosuvastatin (20 mg daily).
(b) (6)
A 77-year-old White male experienced a fatal stroke on Study Day 207. The subject began the study in the aprocitentan
12.5 mg arm and completed Part 1, switching to aprocitentan 25 mg for Part 2 on Study Day 33. On the morning of Day
207, the subject was found in bed with “abrupt clinical signs of deep coma with noisy pathological breathing.” Emergency
responders confirmed death of the subject. The family doctor, who witnessed the subject’s death, said to the relatives that
he diagnosed the cause of death as stroke. An autopsy was not performed. The last available BP value on Day 201 was
128/70 mmHg. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included angina pectoris, chronic cardiac failure, hypertensive
heart disease, mitral valve disease, myocardial ischemia, dyslipidemia, chronic kidney disease, cerebral arteriosclerosis,
and encephalopathy. At baseline, he had a BMI of 35.4kg/m2 and an eGFR of 44 mL/min/1.73 m2. Concomitant
medications at baseline included rosuvastatin (10 mg daily).

(b) (6)

A 79-year-old White male experienced left middle cerebral artery stroke resulting in hospitalization on Study Day 32, and
death on Study Day 37. The subject began the study in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm and completed Part 1, starting Part 2
on Study Day 29. On Study Day 31, the subject was reported as normal by his wife. The next day, she found him on the
bathroom floor, and he was taken to the emergency department 12 hours later, presenting with right-sided weakness, the
inability follow commands, and aphasia. Tissue plasminogen activator was not administered due to being outside the
treatment time criteria. Hemoglobin and hematocrit were within normal ranges, eGFR was stable at 50 mL/min/1.73 m2.
On the same day, the subject was transferred to another hospital for possible mechanical thrombectomy, which ultimately
was not attempted due to the core infarct being too large, and expiration of the treatment time window. The subject was
then discharged to palliative care on Study Day 35 and died on Study Day 37. The last available BP value on Day 26 was
124/72 mmHg. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included chronic kidney disease, aortic arteriosclerosis,
peripheral edema, sleep apnea syndrome, peripheral venous disease, gastroesophageal reflux disease, and
hyperparathyroidism. At baseline, he had a BMI of 36.6 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 47 mL/min/1.73 m2. Concomitant
medications at baseline included acetylsalicylic acid (81 mg daily), omeprazole (20 mg daily), and montelukast sodium
(10 mg daily).

Table 144. Narratives for Adjudicated MACE and MACE-Plus, Safety Population, PRECISION Study
Subject ID
Clinical Information
Part 1
(b) (6)
The 77-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg arm experienced nonfatal ischemic stroke on Study Day 27. The
subject was hospitalized due to sudden dysarthria. A brain CT showed the presence of a hypodense area with blurry borders in
the area of mesencephalon at the left side towards thalamus, which correspond to subacute ischemic changes. Otherwise,
there was no intracranial bleeding or expansive process. An MRI was not performed. The study drug was permanently
discontinued on Day 28 due to ischemic stroke. The event remained unresolved and was assessed as related to the study
drug. Relevant medical conditions were reported as hypertension, previous transient ischemic attack, type 2 diabetes mellitus,
dyslipidemia, and CKD. At baseline, he had a BMI of 29.7 kg/m2, eGFR of 38 mL/min/1.73 m2, and NT-proBNP of 246 pg/mL.
(b) (6)
The 75-year-old White female in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg arm experienced an SAE of subacute right medial occipital lobe
infarct on Study Day 17. The subject noticed symptoms of floaters in both eyes but did not notify the site. On Study Day 48, an
MRI of the head without contrast indicated probable late subacute infarct in the right medial occipital lobe, noted to be at least
several weeks in age, which corresponded with the onset of her visual changes. There was no evidence of hemorrhage or
recent infarct. The study drug was interrupted from Day 38-41. The event remained unresolved and was considered not related
to the study drug. The subject restarted treatment on Study Day 41 and completed Part 2 and Part 3 of the study. Relevant
medical conditions were reported as hypertension (40 years) and peripheral edema. At baseline, she had a BMI of 31 kg/m2
and an eGFR of 62 mL/min/1.73 m2.
(b) (6)
The 72-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 12.5 mg arm experienced a serious event of nonfatal myocardial infarction on
Study Day 19-23. The event resolved with treatment. No action with study drug was taken. The event was assessed as not
related. On Study Day 95, the subject experienced a serious event of nonfatal myocardial infarction related to coronary artery
bypass graft (CABG) surgery, which was performed the day prior. The event resolved with coronarography and bypass. No
action with study drug was taken. The event was assessed as not related. Relevant ongoing medical conditions at Screening
included chronic cardiac failure, angina, coronary artery disease, myocardial ischemia, hypercholesterolemia, peripheral

mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 56-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced a serious event of nonfatal stroke on Study Day 4-23.
The event resolved with sequelae (speech impaired and memory reduced). The study drug was permanently discontinued on
Study Day 15. The event was assessed as not related. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included type 2 diabetes,
dyslipidemia, and tobacco use. At baseline, he had a BMI of 35.9kg/m2 and an eGFR of 100 mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 67-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of pulmonary edema, cardiac failure, and
increased blood pressure on Study Day 8-12. The subject was admitted to the hospital with a diagnosis of heart failure with
preserved ejection fraction (HFpEF) with flash pulmonary edema after experiencing increasing dyspnea. Blood pressure upon
arrival was 220/104 mmHg and chest X-ray showed early pulmonary edema. The subject’s NT-proBNP value during
hospitalization was not reported. The subject recovered after treatment and the event was considered not related to the study
drug. There was no action take with study treatment. On Study Day 23, the subject presented to the emergency department
with severe chest pain and peripheral edema that resolved after treatment with transdermal nitroglycerin and furosemide.
There was no action taken with study treatment and the event was considered not related to the study drug. Ongoing medical
conditions at Screening included type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, history of transient ischemic attack, and peripheral edema. At
baseline, he had a BMI of 38.1kg/m2 and an eGFR of 62 mL/min/1.73 m2.
An 83-year-old White female in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm had an SAE of cardiac failure on Study Day 22-30. The subject
was admitted to the hospital with a diagnosis of worsening of chronic heart failure after experiencing edema and dyspnea since
study Day 11. The study drug was interrupted from Day 22-30 and restarted on Day 31. The subject recovered after treatment
and the event was considered not related to the study drug. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included atrial fibrillation,
chronic heart failure, chronic kidney disease, type 2 diabetes, dyslipidemia, transient ischemic attack, and peripheral arterial
occlusive disease. At baseline, she had a BMI of 37.1kg/m2 and an eGFR of 38 mL/min/1.73 m2.

Part 2
(b) (6)

(b) (6)

The 66-year-old Black male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced a serious event of nonfatal myocardial infarction on
Study Day 41. The subject was hospitalized with a diagnosis of myocardial infarction of mild intensity (non-ST-elevation) after
reporting edema, dyspnea, and chest pain for the prior 4 days. The event resolved after treatment. The study drug was
permanently discontinued on Study Day 41. The event was assessed as not related. Relevant ongoing medical conditions at
Screening included coronary artery disease, left bundle branch block, hypercholesterolemia, peripheral arterial occlusive
disease, diabetic nephropathy, and type 2 diabetes. At baseline, he had a BMI of 30.3 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 72 mL/min/1.73
m2.
The 73-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced a serious event of nonfatal myocardial infarction on
Study Day 181 during hospitalization for a scheduled left femoral thromboendarterectomy of moderate intensity and iliac
stenting. He was transferred to the cardiology unit and underwent a coronarography on Day 186 that revealed trivascular
coronaropathy with occlusion of medio-proximal anterior interventricular artery. The same day, he underwent a coronary
angioplasty plus stent, on common trunk and distal segment. The procedure was uncomplicated and regular; the subject was
discharged on study Day 190. No action was taken with the study drug and the event was assessed as not related to study
treatment. Relevant ongoing medical conditions at Screening included hypercholesterolemia, chronic kidney disease,
peripheral vascular disorder, and type 2 diabetes. At baseline, he had a BMI of 27.8 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 30 mL/min/1.73 m2.

Clinical Information
A 57-year-old Black male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm had an SAE of congestive heart failure exacerbation on Study Day 4675. The subject was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 68 with a diagnosis of congestive heart failure exacerbation after
experiencing a three-week history of increasing dyspnea, peripheral edema, and cough. The subject recovered after treatment
and the event was considered not related to the study drug. During hospitalization on study Day 68, the subject experience
acute kidney failure of moderate intensity, which was assessed as related to study treatment. The study drug was permanently
discontinued the same day. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included chronic left ventricular failure, type 2 diabetes,
dyslipidemia, chronic kidney disease, diabetic retinopathy, and peripheral edema. At baseline, he had a BMI of 42.9kg/m2 and
an eGFR of 59 mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 66-year-old White female in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of congestive heart failure on Study Day 3947. The subject was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 39 after experiencing a two-day history of dyspnea. Chest CTA
showed bilateral pleural effusion and pulmonary edema secondary to heart failure. There was no pulmonary embolism or signs
of infiltrates. On Day 41, transthoracic echocardiogram showed an ejection fraction of 50-55% with grade 3/4 diastolic
dysfunction and right ventricular systolic pressure of 51 mmHg. A nuclear stress test showed an intermediate risk stress test
due to left ventricular dilatation and transient ischemic dilatation, most consistent with either a dilated cardiomyopathy or could
be seen with multivessel coronary disease and balanced ischemia. The subject recovered after treatment and the event was
assessed as not related to the study drug. The study drug was permanently discontinued on study Day 39. Ongoing medical
conditions at Screening included left ventricular hypertrophy, type 2 diabetes, and dyslipidemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of
34.6 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 91 mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 70-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of heart failure on Study Day 53-55. The subject
was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 53 after experiencing a 2-week history of dyspnea and peripheral edema. A 2D
echocardiogram revealed a serious event of grade 2 left ventricular diastolic dysfunction of moderate intensity with preserved
ejection fraction. The subject recovered after treatment and the event was assessed as not related to the study drug. No action
was taken with the study treatment. Of note, the hospitalization was complicated by diagnosis of COVID-19 of severe intensity
and the subject remained in the hospital until Study Day 63. Ongoing medical conditions at Screening included chronic kidney
disease, coronary artery disease, type 2 diabetes, and dyslipidemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of 26.7 kg/m2 and an eGFR of
37 mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 72-year-old Black male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of pulmonary edema, congestive heart failure,
and hypertensive urgency on Study Day 134 to 137 that led to drug interruption for the hypertensive urgency. The subject was
admitted to the hospital with a diagnosis of congestive heart failure, pulmonary edema in the setting of hypertensive urgency,
acute kidney injury on chronic kidney disease, and atrial flutter. Blood pressure upon arrival was reported as 209/60 mmHg
and chest X-ray showed enlarged cardiac silhouette and pulmonary vasculature congestion. The subject recovered after
treatment with intravenous (IV) furosemide, nitrates, and hydralazine. The study treatment was interrupted on Day 137 due to
the hypertensive urgency. The event was considered not related to study drug. Prior to the study (at screening), the subject’s
individual background antihypertensive therapy included SBAT and hydralazine (50 mg twice daily) which was discontinued at
that time, per study protocol. The subject was restarted on study treatment on Study Day 147, completing the study Parts 2
and 3 in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm as scheduled.
A 79-year-old White female in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of cardiac failure on Study Day 151-157. The
subject was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 151 after experiencing a 4-week history of night dyspnea and peripheral

A 63-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced a serious event of nonfatal myocardial infarction on
Study Day 360-371. The subject was hospitalized with a diagnosis of myocardial infarction (type 1 with ST segment elevation)
and cardiogenic shock after a 3-day history of chest pain and irregular heartbeat. No action was taken with the study drug and
the event was assessed as not related to study treatment. Relevant ongoing medical conditions at Screening included left
ventricular hypertrophy, cerebral ischemia, and hypercholesterolemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of 36.3 kg/m2 and an eGFR of

A 63-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of unstable angina and aortic stenosis on Study
Day 312-332. The subject was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 313 after experiencing chest pain. Coronary angiography
showed evidence of normal coronary vessels. Echocardiography revealed worsened aortic stenosis on native bicuspid valve,
maximum gradient >80 mmHg, with surgical indication. The subject recovered after aortic valve replacement and the event
was assessed as not related to the study drug. Study treatment was permanently discontinued on Study Day 312. Ongoing
medical conditions at Screening included cardiac failure, peripheral edema, chronic kidney disease, coronary artery disease,
type 2 diabetes, history of ischemic stroke, and dyslipidemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of 46.1 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 33
mL/min/1.73 m2.
A 75-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of acute diastolic heart failure on Study Day 315317. The subject was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 315 for volume overload with mild pulmonary edema and lower
extremity edema after experiencing a 10-day history of mild peripheral edema. Chest X-ray showed signs of congestion with
small pleural effusions. A second local B-natriuretic peptide (Day 136) was 217 pg/mL. Echocardiography revealed mildly
dilated right atrium and increased left atrial volume and volume index; a trace to mild degree of mitral regurgitation; mild
pulmonary hypertension with estimated right ventricular systolic pressure of 40 mmHg. CT pulmonary angiogram showed no
acute pulmonary embolism, minimal interstitial edema, trace bilateral pleural effusions. The subject recovered after treatment
and the event was assessed as not related to the study drug. No action was taken with the study treatment. Ongoing medical
conditions at Screening included type 2 diabetes and dyslipidemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of 34.3 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 87
mL/min/1.73 m2.

Clinical Information
A 62-year-old White male in the aprocitentan 25 mg arm experienced an SAE of dyspnea on Study Day 277-282. The subject
was admitted to the hospital on Study Day 277 for fluid retention and decompensation of heart failure. The subject was
previously diagnosed with pulmonary neoplasm and was taking chemotherapy at the time of hospitalization. The subject
recovered from the dyspnea and heart failure exacerbation after treatment and the event was assessed as not related to the
study drug. The study treatment had already been permanently discontinued prior to the event on Study Day 276. Ongoing
medical conditions at Screening included cardiac failure, type 2 diabetes and dyslipidemia. At baseline, he had a BMI of
32.2 kg/m2 and an eGFR of 91 mL/min/1.73 m2.
